Kansas State’s Zoonotic Disease Research Hub Lands $11.4 Million NIH Renewal - Global Biodefense

Kansas State’s Zoonotic Disease Research Hub Lands $11.4 Million NIH Renewal - Global Biodefense
新闻简报Kansas State University's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID) has received a $11.4 million renewal from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) through 2031. This funding supports the center's research on high-consequence pathogens, including mpox virus, and aims to build scientific workforce and laboratory infrastructure. CEZID, housed in the College of Veterinary Medicine, is part of a regional scientific network that includes collaborations with the University of Kansas and the University of Kansas Medical Center. The center has already submitted over 100 research proposals and received 30 awards totaling approximately $18 million in external funding. The renewal ensures continued efforts to study how pathogens move between animals and humans and how they can be stopped. The ongoing H5N1 situation in U.S. dairy herds highlights the potential for animal health problems to become human health emergencies, underscoring the importance of CEZID's research.
查看新闻五要素
时间
Close Menu Facebook X (Twitter) Instagram Facebook Instagram Threads Subscribe Featured Ebola Outbreak Avian Influenza Measles Resurgence Vaccine News Events Advertisement Contracts + Grants Kansas State’s Zoonotic Disease Research Hub Lands $11.4 Million NIH Renewal Six years of momentum: K-State's infectious disease center enters its next phase By Global Biodefense StaffAugust 29, 2026 Kansas State University researchers examine a sample as part of the university's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID), which recently secured $11.4 million in continued NIH funding through 2031.
地点与对象
堪萨斯州立大学,位于美国,特别是在曼哈顿校区,重点关注区域农业走廊以及与堪萨斯大学和堪萨斯大学医学中心的研究合作。
事件
Kansas State University's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID) received a $11.4 million renewal from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) for its research on high-consequence pathogens, including mpox virus, through 2031. The funding supports the continuation of research activities and infrastructure.
规模、影响与风险
The renewal ensures the continuation of CEZID's research efforts, which have already submitted over 100 research proposals and received 30 awards totaling approximately $18 million in external funding. The research includes studies on mpox virus and its interactions with hosts, as well as other zoonotic pathogens like H5N1 avian influenza and SARS-CoV-2. The scale of the research is significant, with shared laboratory cores and a regional scientific network.
应对、状态与不确定性
NIH 的资金更新表明对 CEZID 的研究基础设施和科学管道的持续支持。该中心的工作包括研究 猴痘病毒 及其导致人类健康紧急情况的可能性,正如美国当前的 H5N1 疫情所表明的那样。该研究是了解和减轻人畜共患威胁的更广泛努力的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Kansas State’s Zoonotic Disease Research Hub Lands $11.4 Million NIH Renewal - Global Biodefense

News Brief
Kansas State University's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID) has received a $11.4 million renewal from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) through 2031. This funding supports the center's research on high-consequence pathogens, including mpox virus, and aims to build scientific workforce and laboratory infrastructure. CEZID, housed in the College of Veterinary Medicine, is part of a regional scientific network that includes collaborations with the University of Kansas and the University of Kansas Medical Center. The center has already submitted over 100 research proposals and received 30 awards totaling approximately $18 million in external funding. The renewal ensures continued efforts to study how pathogens move between animals and humans and how they can be stopped. The ongoing H5N1 situation in U.S. dairy herds highlights the potential for animal health problems to become human health emergencies, underscoring the importance of CEZID's research.
Original source text (English)
Close Menu Facebook X (Twitter) Instagram Facebook Instagram Threads Subscribe Featured Ebola Outbreak Avian Influenza Measles Resurgence Vaccine News Events Advertisement Contracts + Grants Kansas State’s Zoonotic Disease Research Hub Lands $11.4 Million NIH Renewal Six years of momentum: K-State's infectious disease center enters its next phase By Global Biodefense StaffAugust 29, 2026 Kansas State University researchers examine a sample as part of the university's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID), which recently secured $11.4 million in continued NIH funding through 2031. The center, directed by Juergen A. Richt, studies high-consequence pathogens including H5N1 avian influenza, African swine fever, mpox, and SARS-CoV-2. Photo credit: K-State University. Share Facebook LinkedIn Reddit Email The boundary between animal health and human health has never been more consequential. Diseases that jump from animals to people, from H5N1 avian influenza spreading through dairy cattle to emerging bacterial threats, represent one of the most persistent and unpredictable challenges in global public health. Sustained institutional capacity to study these pathogens before they become crises is exactly what federal research investment is meant to build. Kansas State University has secured $11.4 million in continued National Institutes of Health funding to support the Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID), through April 2031. The NIH award extends the center into its second five-year phase and signals confidence in the research infrastructure and scientific pipeline K-State has built since CEZID launched in 2020. CEZID is housed in K-State’s College of Veterinary Medicine and directed by Juergen A. Richt, a Regents and university distinguished professor whose work spans high-consequence pathogens including H5N1 influenza, African swine fever virus, mpox, and SARS-CoV-2. The center’s mission is straightforward: build the scientific workforce and shared laboratory infrastructure needed to understand how dangerous pathogens cause disease, how they move between animals and humans, and how they can be stopped. K-State sits within a major agricultural corridor and operates some of the country’s most capable high-containment research facilities, including the Biosecurity Research Institute, a BSL-3 and agricultural BSL-3 facility on the Manhattan campus. The nearby National Bio and Agro-Defense Facility further concentrates expertise and infrastructure in the region. CEZID links the university’s infectious disease research to this broader ecosystem, creating a regional hub with national reach. In its first five years, CEZID investigators submitted over 100 research proposals and received 30 awards totaling approximately $18 million in external funding. Phase 2 will pursue four research tracks examining virulence factors and host-pathogen interactions across a range of pathogens. Current projects include studies of Borrelia burgdorferi, the bacterium responsible for Lyme disease; how obesity alters influenza severity through multi-omics analysis; sporulation mechanisms in Clostridioides difficile, a major source of hospital-acquired infection; and a novel coronavirus vaccine strategy using genetic deoptimization to render viruses harmless. Two shared laboratory cores will continue operating throughout Phase 2. The Animal Model/Pathology Core provides BSL-2 and BSL-3 research capabilities, animal model development, and pathology expertise. The Molecular and Cellular Biology Core offers next-generation sequencing, flow cytometry, and spatial transcriptomics. Both cores were integrated into K-State’s Biomedical Core Facilities during Phase 1, extending access to investigators across the university and the broader regional research community. The Regional Scientific Network, which connects CEZID investigators with collaborators at the University of Kansas and the University of Kansas Medical Center, will expand as well. The ongoing H5N1 situation in U.S. dairy herds, which CEZID researchers have studied directly, illustrates how quickly an animal health problem can become a potential human health emergency. The five-year renewal ensures K-State retains and deepens one of the country’s more capable regional centers for this work. Sources and further information: CEZID receives $11.4 million in continued NIH funding – K-State Today This content was researched and sourced by Global Biodefense editors and reported with Claude AI assistance for drafting and editing. Location and population
Kansas State University, located in the United States, specifically in the Manhattan campus, with a focus on the regional agricultural corridor and research collaborations with the University of Kansas and the University of Kansas Medical Center.
Event
Kansas State University's Center on Emerging and Zoonotic Infectious Diseases (CEZID) received a $11.4 million renewal from the National Institutes of Health (NIH) for its research on high-consequence pathogens, including mpox virus, through 2031. The funding supports the continuation of research activities and infrastructure.
Scale, impact and risk
The renewal ensures the continuation of CEZID's research efforts, which have already submitted over 100 research proposals and received 30 awards totaling approximately $18 million in external funding. The research includes studies on mpox virus and its interactions with hosts, as well as other zoonotic pathogens like H5N1 avian influenza and SARS-CoV-2. The scale of the research is significant, with shared laboratory cores and a regional scientific network.
Response, status and uncertainty
The NIH funding renewal indicates ongoing support for CEZID's research infrastructure and scientific pipeline. The center's work includes studying mpox virus and its potential to cause human health emergencies, as seen in the ongoing H5N1 situation in U.S. The research is part of a broader effort to understand and mitigate zoonotic threats.

猴痘|39岁男子确诊 潜伏期曾在本地与两名陌生男子有高风险接触 - 香港01

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报香港卫生防护中心于2026年8月29日确认一宗新的猴痘病例,涉及一名未接种疫苗的39岁男子,他在潜伏期与两名身份不明的男子高风险接触后出现发烧、皮疹和淋巴结肿大。该患者目前在玛嘉烈医院隔离,病情稳定,是自 2022 年全港 暴发 开始以来报告的首例本地感染病例。当局正在追踪接触者并调查与先前病例的潜在联系,同时敦促高危人群(包括有多个性伴或最近感染性传播感染的人群)在非预约诊所接受 疫苗接种。尚未发现与早期当地病例的流行病学联系。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件日期:2026-08-24 至 2026-08-29;发表日期:2026-08-29 16:15(香港时间)
地点与对象
中国香港特别行政区。受影响者:39岁,未接种疫苗的男性。在玛格丽特公主医院进行隔离和 治疗。
事件
卫生署卫生防护中心报告一宗确诊的 猴痘 个案。病人在香港与两名身份不明的男子进行高风险接触后,于潜伏期内出现病征。
规模、影响与风险
新增1例猴痘确诊病例;患者被隔离,病情稳定。2022年以来香港累计猴痘病例:97例(77例本地病例,20例输入),全部为男性。大多数病例与高风险性行为有关。没有证据表明与先前的当地病例有流行病学联系。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生防护中心正在进行流行病学调查和接触者追踪。病例已向世界卫生组织报告。卫生当局敦促高危人群(多伴侣、性工作者、近期性传播感染史)在指定诊所接种疫苗,无需预约。如果出现 猴痘 症状,建议公众就医,并避免接触皮疹或体液的活动。

Original title (Chinese)
猴痘|39岁男子确诊 潜伏期曾在本地与两名陌生男子有高风险接触 - 香港01
News Brief
Hong Kong’s Centre for Health Protection confirmed a new mpox case on 29 August 2026 involving a 39-year-old unvaccinated man who developed fever, rash, and lymphadenopathy after high-risk contact with two unknown men during his incubation period. The patient, now isolated at Princess Margaret Hospital in stable condition, is the first locally acquired case reported since the territory-wide outbreak began in 2022. Authorities are tracing contacts and investigating potential links to prior cases, while urging high-risk groups—including those with multiple partners or recent sexually transmitted infections—to receive vaccination at walk-in clinics. No epidemiological connection to earlier local cases has been identified.

本港今日(29日)新增一宗猴痘确诊个案。卫生署卫生防护中心表示,一名39岁未曾接种猴痘疫苗的男子,于潜伏期内曾与两名陌生男子发生高风险接触,其后出现发烧及全身出疹等症状,检测后证实对猴痘病毒呈阳性反应,目前在玛嘉烈医院隔离治疗,情况稳定。防护中心正进行流行病学调查,并尝试追踪相关接触者。 卫生署卫生防护中心。(资料图片) 卫生防护中心指,该名39岁男子自8月24日起出现发烧及颈部淋巴结肿大,其后口腔出现溃疡,面部、躯干、四肢及生殖器官亦陆续出疹。他于8月26日因自身疾病前往伊利沙伯医院覆诊,即日被安排留院隔离及接受治疗。病人经公共卫生化验服务处采样检测后,今日证实确诊猴痘,并于同日转送至玛嘉烈医院继续接受隔离治疗。 流行病学调查显示,该名病人於潜伏期内曾在本港与两名陌生男子有高风险接触。防护中心指,暂未有资料显示今次个案与本港早前录得的其他猴痘确诊个案存在流行病学关连,中心正全力追查及联系曾与其有高风险接触的人士,并会将个案通报世界卫生组织。 防护中心资料显示,本港自2022年至今累计录得97宗猴痘个案,包括77宗本地个案及20宗输入个案,所有患者均为男性。调查亦发现绝大部份个案均曾进行高风险性行为,包括与陌生人发生性行为,或在没有使用安全套的情况下进行性行为。 猴痘:图为美联社发布日期为2022年5月20日的照片,显示1997年刚果民主共和国一名猴痘病人的手部皮肤状况。(AP) 卫生署呼吁高风险目标群组,包括拥有多名性伴侣、性工作者或过去12个月有性传播感染史的人士尽快接种猴痘疫苗。相关人士可直接前往卫生署辖下的社会卫生科诊所或油麻地综合治疗中心接种,无须预约;九龙湾综合治疗中心以及伊利沙伯医院和玛嘉烈医院的特别内科诊所亦会为到诊者提供接种服务。 当局提醒,猴痘一般不会经空气、飞沫或日常社交接触传播。市民若发现皮疹、发烧、发冷、淋巴结肿大、疲乏或肌肉痛等猴痘症状,应尽快求医,并停止参与任何可能接触到皮疹或体液的活动。 猴痘|39岁男确诊 潜伏期曾在泰国与陌生男子高风险接触猴痘|32岁男确诊 潜伏期曾在港与多名陌生男子有高风险性行为猴痘|31岁男确诊 潜伏期曾在港与多名陌生男子有性行为猴痘|41岁本港男子确诊 潜伏期独往台湾旅游有高风险接触猴痘|44岁男确诊 曾在家中有高风险接触 中心联系涉有接触人士猴痘|增一宗输入个案 26岁男子潜伏期泰国旅游 有高风险接触猴痘|同志桑拿“胡同”群组增至5人 最新患者5.23到访有性接触猴痘|43岁男确诊 发病前曾到爆疫同志桑拿“胡同”及在酒店群P 2 Cards Offering $200 Bonuses(CardCritics) Top Tech Investor A confirmed mpox case reported by the Centre for Health Protection (CHP) under the Department of Health. The patient developed symptoms during the incubation period after high-risk contact with two unknown men in Hong Kong.
